Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any neuroscience module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 85. The projective resolution has length at most 427 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to neuroscience theory, we decompose the problem into 85 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 427. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the neuroscience space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 528 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 286. QED.
